- Monitor score.

Helper functions:
- safe_normalize(p): p=np.asarray(p,float); p=np.clip(p,0,1000); S=p.sum(); if S<=0: uniform; else p/=S; return p.
- fast_conv(p, L): P=np.fft.rfft(p, L); c=np.fft.irfft(P*P, L)[:M]; c[np.abs(c)<1e-15]=0; return np.maximum(c,0) maybe. But for gradient, small negative not issue. Use c = c.copy(); c = np.where(c>0,c,0). 
- fast_score(p): c=fast_conv; return 2*n*max(c). Note evaluator score uses sum(a)^2; if p sum1, same. If p values sum not exactly 1 due underflow, normalize.
- gradient_from_weights(w, p, L): p_rev=p[::-1]; W=np.fft.rfft(w,L); Pr=np.fft.rfft(p_rev,L); g_raw=np.fft.irfft(W*Pr,L)[n-1:2n-1]; return 2*g_raw. Wait w length M. Our sum w2p2+w1p1+w0p0 yes. Good. i=1 index3: m0 w3 p2; m1 w2 p1; m2 w1 p0. Desired k-i valid j: k=1..3? ∑ w_k p_{k-1}: w1p0+w2p1+w3p2. Good. i=2 index4: w2p0+w3p1+w4p2. Good. So correct.

Let's consider implementing a continuous optimization instead of mirror descent: We can optimize p on simplex with projected gradient of max c. Since p0 has all positive, zeros not issue. Projected gradient:
   grad_i = 2∑ w_k p_{k-i}
   p_new = p - step * grad
   project onto simplex: find threshold τ such that max(p-τ,0) sums 1. This is standard EW algorithm O(n log n). This directly moves p in Euclidean geometry, not mirror. Which is better? Mirror descent respects positive and can make multiplicative changes; projected gradient may zero entries. For nonnegative simplex, both valid. Mirror descent with softmax is unconstrained and may be smoother. Projected gradient step size can be larger? Since grad ~1/n, step needs O(n) to move sum; e.g., step 1. Maybe use `p -= step * grad` with step ~10. But could violate nonneg. Mirror descent likely better.

Could use "exponentiated gradient" for maximizing concave? We minimize convex? φ smooth. Softmax natural.

Let's design run_adam carefully to avoid too many small improvements. Pseudocode top-level:
```
def run_adam(v, m, u, t, n, L, deadline, rng, lr, q, mix, best_p, best_fast, best_exact, last_exact):
    beta1=0.9; beta2=0.999; eps=1e-8
    no_improve=0; last_best_iter=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        p = softmax_safe(v)
        c = fft_convolution(p, L)
        cmax = float(c.max())
        score = 2.0*n*cmax
        if score < best_fast - 1e-12:
            best_p = p.copy(); best_fast = score; no_improve=0
            last_exact = ... # maybe exact if score significantly better than best_exact
        else:
            no_improve += 1
        if no_improve > 2000:
             # perturb v
             v = v + rng.normal(0, 0.02, n)
             # reset? maybe reduce lr
             lr = max(0.01, lr*0.7)
             q = min(q*2, 128)
             no_improve=0
        # build weights
        if cmax <= 0: break
        scale = 10.0 / cmax
        # prevent underflow: uvals = np.clip((c-cmax)*scale, -700, 0)
        w_soft = np.exp((c - cmax) * scale)
        sw = w_soft.sum()
        if sw <=0: w_soft = np.zeros_like(c); w_soft[np.argmax(c)]=1
        else: w_soft /= sw
        w_lin = c / c.sum()
        w = mix*w_soft + (1-mix)*w_lin
        ws = w.sum(); if ws>0: w/=ws
        g = gradient_from_weights(w, p, L, n)
        g *= float(n)
        # normalize gradient to unit max? To make lr independent of scale:
        gmax = np.max(np.abs(g))
        if gmax > 1e-12:
            g = g / gmax
        # This makes update O(lr). Is that good? It loses magnitude info; but direction same. Since grad direction depends on weights; scaling by max ok for mirror descent? The Lipschitz scale changes. Use lr around 0.01-0.1.
        t +=1
        m = beta1*m + (1-beta1)*g
        u = beta2*u + (1-beta2)*(g*g)
        mhat = m/(1-beta1**t)
        uhat = u/(1-beta2**t)
        v = v - lr * mhat / (np.sqrt(uhat) + eps)
        v = v - v.mean()
        rng_v = v.max()-v.min()
        if not np.isfinite(v).all() or rng_v > 30:
             if not finite: break
             v = (v - v.mean())*(30/rng_v)
        # Adaptive lr
        if best_fast < last_best_score? We can track.
    return v,m,u,t,best_p,best_fast,best_exact,last_exact
```
If we normalize g to max 1, lr is step in v units. For p0, desired v change O(1) over maybe 100 iterations -> lr 0.01. But Adam's uhat ~1 for active coords, less for inactive, so some coords move faster. Good. Start lr=0.05? Maybe too big. Let's start lr=0.03. Adaptive: if improving, lr=min(0.2, lr*1.01); else lr=max(0.005, lr*0.98).
However if we normalize g each iter, Adam's second moment can be ~1 for all, so update similar to normalized gradient descent. Might be okay.

But normalizing by max could ignore that gradient max maybe should be larger when c larger? lr adapts.

Alternative scale by n only (grad ~1/n *n=O(1)), no max normalize. For one-sided p0, grad_unit max maybe? Let's estimate weights sum1, p_i~C/√i. For i=0, g_unit = n*2∑w p ≈n*(1.4/n)=1.4. For other i, smaller maybe. So grad max ~O(1). No normalize needed. Use lr=0.02-0.1. General p may have grad max different; normalize safety. Let's use `g *= float(n)` then if gmax > 0: g = (g / gmax) * (np.mean(np.abs(g)))? Or just normalize to max 1. I think normalize to max 1 simplifies lr. # compute p_candidate after update

if score_cand < best_fast: update best

The LP: max sum g s.t. f*g≤M, g≥0. We can solve its dual approximately using multiplicative weights for covering. Since to get a direction g, maybe we can solve primal by projected gradient? The feasible set linear; objective linear. We can use simple heuristic: start g = f (or uniform). Repeatedly find violated most? Actually maximize sum g; constraints A g≤M. We can perform coordinate ascent: for each i, g_i can be increased until some constraint tight. We can solve in O(n^2) per full pass. Let's derive.
Given g≥0, objective increase. Constraint k: ∑_{i} f_i g_{k-i} ≤ M. If we increase g_j by δ, constraints k∈[j, j+n-1] increase by f_{k-j} δ. Max δ = min_{k valid} (M - current_k)/(f_{k-j}) (positive). Coordinate ascent: for each j, set g_j = g_j + δ (to one tight). Repeat until no increase. This is a greedy submodular? Since objective linear and constraints monotone, coordinate ascent to local optimum (not global but good). Complexity per coordinate O(active constraints) up to n; full pass O(n^2)=6.4M, okay maybe 5 passes. We can reset or keep. This gives a feasible g max sum maybe close to LP. This is much lighter and no solver! Let's explore.
Primal variables g nonnegative. Constraints A_k = ∑ f_i g_{k-i} ≤ M. Start g=0 or f? If g=0, objective 0. Coordinate ascent can fill. If start g=f (feasible since A f=f*f≤M), objective 1. Then increase coordinates subject to slack. This finds an extreme-ish point. It may be the LP optimum if the constraint matrix has some property? It is a polymatroid? The feasible set {g≥0: A g ≤ M 1} with A nonnegative; maximizing linear objective. This is a linear program over a polymatroid? If the rank function submodular, greedy coordinate yields optimum? The constraints are sums over intervals weighted by f. Not exactly submodular. But coordinate ascent heuristic may be good.
Can we do better: The LP optimum can be found by water-filling: We want allocate mass to g_i where they cause least constraint tightness. Since A constraints are convolution with f. The dual might be easier.

Coordinate ascent algorithm details:
- Precompute for each k current constraint value slacks = M - A_k (length M).
- For each j (position), find max δ: δ_j = min_{k in [j,j+n-1]} slacks[k] / f_{k-j} where f_{k-j}>0. Allowing δ=0 if slack negative (should not). Then update g_j += δ. Update slacks[k] -= f_{k-j} δ for k in range.
Doing this sequentially with slacking in-place changes constraints; later coordinates may have less. This is greedy; order matters. We can repeat cycles and perhaps random order. Objective sum g. Since f positive, f denominators. Complexity each j min over up to n slacks/f. Naive O(n^2) per coordinate? Wait for each j, min over k range length up to n: O(n), total O(n^2) per pass. 6.4M operations per pass. In Python loops too slow. But can vectorize? Need for each j compute min of f-index shifted slacks. This is a sliding window minimum of elementwise division slacks/f. For a given j, denominator f_{k-j} = f_{index}. We need min over k in [j,j+n-1] of slack[k]/f_{k-j}. Could compute by looping k for each j in Python not okay. But n=2535, n^2=6.4M; if done with numpy inner vector operations per j (slice division and min) 2535 calls of length ~2500 -> 6.4M numpy ops, maybe 0.1-0.5s per pass in C with overhead 2535* maybe 0.5s. A few passes possible. This could produce LP-like directions.
- After update g_j, update slacks slice: for t=0..len-1, slacks[j+t] -= f[t]*δ_j. Vectorized slice: `slacks[start:stop] -= f[valid]*delta`. Each j O(n) again. Total pass O(n^2) but vectorized slices; 2535 slices length avg 1250 => 3M operations? Actually update covers each constraint multiple times total n^2. Could be ~1s per pass. 10 passes 10s. Good! This is promising and pure numpy.

Greedy direction implementation top-level:
```
def greedy_direction(f, max_orders=10, rng=None, time_limit=None):
    n=len(f); M=2*n-1
    c=conv_fft(f,L) # need L passed
    Mval = c.max()
    if Mval <=0: return f,1.0
    slacks_base = Mval - c
    best_g=np.zeros(n); best_sum=0.0
    # orders: sorted by f ascending, random permutations
    for order in orders:
       slacks = slacks_base.copy()
       g=np.zeros(n)
       for j in order:
           ratio = slacks[j:j+n] * inv_f  # inf where f=0
           finite = np.isfinite(ratio)
           if finite.any():
               delta = float(np.min(ratio[finite]))
           else: delta=0
           if delta > 1e-14:
              # numerical: if due to negative slacks min negative, delta=0
              if delta <0: delta=0
              g[j] = delta # since start 0; g[j] += delta
              slacks[j:j+n] -= f[:n] * delta
       s=g.sum()
       if s>best_sum: best...
       if time... break
    return best_g/best_sum if best_sum>0 else f
```

Exact greedy direction update:
```
def try_direction(p, rng, L, n, start_time, deadline, max_orders=20):
    c = fft_conv(p,L); Mval=c.max(); slacks=Mval-c
    inv_f = ...
    best_dir=p; best_sum=0
    orders = [np.arange(n)[::-1]? sorted? random]
    for order in orders:
       slacks=slacks_base.copy(); g=zeros(n)
       # maybe passes=2, frac=1.0 or 0.7
       for pass in range(passes):
          for j in order:
             ratio = slacks[j:j+n] * inv_f
             delta = np.min(ratio)
             if delta > min_delta:
                if frac < 1: delta *= frac * rng.random? 
                g[j] += delta
                slacks[j:j+n] -= f * delta
       s=g.sum(); if s>best_sum: best_dir=g/s
    return best_dir, best_sum
```
But if we allow passes with same order, after first pass all g_j set to max given slacks at process time. If frac=1, no later δ. If frac<1, some slack remains; second pass can increase. Good. Use number of passes=2 and random frac 0.6-0.9 to approximate optimum. Starting g zero.
Could also use order sorted by f ascending? Since coefficient pattern independent of j? f_j itself doesn't affect ratio except through slacks updated from previous g. The position j's own f not in coefficient except later? The coefficient for g_j in constraint k is f_{k-j}, independent of f_j. The effect of increasing g_j is to reduce slacks by f_t δ over window length n. The "cost" of increasing g_j is the same vector f shifted. The starting position determines which constraints get hit (their slack level). So order should process positions where the shifted f has more slack. We can heuristic: at each step, choose j with largest δ (most headroom) rather than fixed order. This is a greedy maximum marginal gain per unit? Since objective per unit δ is 1, and δ for each j current. We can repeatedly pick j with largest positive δ, take some fraction. But computing all δ each step O(n^2) and picking O(n) steps -> too slow. Fixed random order okay.

Maybe use sorted by initial δ: compute δ_base_j = min(slacks_base[j:j+n]*inv_f) for all j? That is an all-positions min erosion, O(n^2). Then process descending δ. This yields a good order. We can compute δ_base in one loop n times (same cost as one pass) but no updates. Then sort. Could be beneficial. But one pass with fixed order already O(n^2); adding base O(n^2) maybe okay. However if we process with updates, initial δ changes. Use dynamic? Maybe overkill.

Top-level helper functions:
```
import numpy as np
import time

def _normalize_to_one(p, n_fallback=2535):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    if p.ndim != 1:
        p = p.reshape(-1)
    if p.size == 0:
        p = np.ones(n_fallback)
    p = np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)
    p = np.clip(p, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(p.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-12:
        p = np.ones(p.size if p.size>0 else n_fallback, dtype=np.float64)
        s = float(p.sum())
    p = p / s
    return p

def _fft_conv(p, L):
    P = np.fft.rfft(p, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(P * P, L)
    # linear conv length? We only need up to call? Full length L. Return.
    # Clip tiny negatives
    # For speed, don't copy? return c
    c = np.where(c > 0, c, 0.0)
    return c

def _score_from_conv(c, n):
    return 2.0 * float(n) * float(c.max())

def _gradient_from_weights(w, p, L, n):
    pr = p[::-1]
    W = np.fft.rfft(w, L)
    P = np.fft.rfft(pr, L)
    g = np.fft.irfft(W * P, L)
    return 2.0 * g[n-1:2*n-1]
```
Need L enough for w*p_rev: len w=2n-1, p_rev=n => 3n-2. We choose L >= 3n-1. For p conv, L same okay. In `_fft_conv`, c length L, max may include wrap? No, L >=2n-1 so no wrap, c after 2n-1 zeros (maybe numerical). Good.

`_softmax(v, floor=1e-300)`:
```
def _softmax(v):
    v = np.asarray(v, float)
    v = v - float(np.max(v))
    # To avoid underflow, clip to [-745,0]
    e = np.exp(np.clip(v, -745.0, 0.0))
    s=e.sum()
    if s <=0 or not finite:
        return np.full(v.shape, 1.0/v.size)
    return e/s
```
Since v can be large negative, exp underflow to0; if all underflow, uniform.

`_make_weights(c, mix)`:
```
def _make_weights(c, mix):
    cmax = float(c.max())
    if cmax <= 0:
       return np.ones(c.size)/c.size
    scale = 8.0 / cmax  # max u=8
    u = (c - cmax) * scale
    u = np.clip(u, -745.0, 0.0)
    w_soft = np.exp(u)
    sw = w_soft.sum()
    if sw <=0 or not finite:
       w_soft = np.zeros(c.size); w_soft[np.argmax(c)] = 1
    else: w_soft /= sw
    csum = float(c.sum())
    if csum >0:
       w_lin = c / csum
    else: uniform
    w = mix*w_soft + (1-mix)*w_lin
    wsum = w.sum()
    if wsum <=0: uniform
    else: w /= wsum
    return w
```
Scale 10/cmax maybe. `mix` from 0.3 to 0.9.

Greedy direction top-level:
```
def _greedy_direction(p, L, n, rng, max_orders=20, passes=2, frac=1.0, timeout=None, start=None):
    c = _fft_conv(p, L)
    Mval = float(c.max())
    if Mval <=0: return p.copy(), 1.0
    slacks_base = Mval - c
    slacks_base = np.where(slacks_base >0, slacks_base, 0.0)
    # inv p
    invp = np.empty(n)
    pos = p > 1e-300
    invp[pos] = 1.0/p[pos]
    invp[~pos] = np.inf
    best_g = p.copy()
    best_sum = 0.0
    orders=[]
    # add sorted by position? tail first
    idx=np.arange(n)
    orders.append(idx[::-1].copy())  # tail first
    orders.append(idx.copy())
    # random orders
    for _ in range(max_orders-2):
        orders.append(rng.permutation(n))
    # maybe order by p ascending? Since small p positions may have more slack? 
    orders.append(np.argsort(p)[:n][::-1]? actually ascending p = small p first)
```
For each order, to avoid too long, check time.
```
    for order in orders:
        slacks = slacks_base.copy()
        g = np.zeros(n)
        # use maybe shuffle order each pass? 
        for pass_no in range(passes):
            # If frac<1, maybe randomize order? use same.
            for j in order:
                ratio = slacks[j:j+n] * invp
                # if any nan? set inf
                if not np.isfinite(ratio).all(): ratio = np.where(np.isfinite(ratio), ratio, np.inf)
                d = float(np.min(ratio))
                if d > 1e-15:
                    if frac < 1.0:
                        d *= frac * (0.7 + 0.6*rng.random())
                    g[j] += d
                    slacks[j:j+n] -= p[:j? wait p offset f_t = p[t] because coefficient f_{k-j}=p_t. Yes p[:n] independent.
        s = float(g.sum())
        if s > best_sum:
            best_sum=s; best_g = g/s
        if timeout and time.monotonic()>timeout: break
    return best_g, best_sum
```
But `p[:n]` is p itself. Use `pf=p`. For j, `slacks[j:j+n] -= p * d`. This is a slice of slacks and p full length; okay.
Need ensure slacks remains nonnegative; due floating, can become -1e-15. After update, if min<0, clip? If we clip, feasibility approximate. We can do `slacks[j:j+n] = np.maximum(slacks[j:j+n], 0.0)` after update? But ratio min used current negative? At next j, min could be negative, d negative -> skip. We can set ratio = np.maximum(slacks, 0)*invp before min. But if slacks negative for some constraints, feasible violated; by taking d=min over positive, we might not fix negative. Better maintain `slacks = np.maximum(slacks, 0)` after each update? This keeps feasible but may overestimate slacks for constraints that violated; direction may be slightly infeasible. Fine for heuristic. We can initialize and after update `slacks[j:j+n] = np.maximum(slacks[j:j+n], 0.0)`. But computing min over nonnegative. This may allow more than exact LP but okay if we line search convex combo (fast score will reject if bad). 
To speed, ratio = slacks_slice * invp. If invp inf and slacks 0 -> nan; handle with `np.errstate` and `ratio = np.where(np.isfinite(ratio), ratio, np.inf)`.
Since p positive mostly, invp finite.

But greedy direction from 0 with passes and fraction might produce g sum <1. If best_sum<1, maybe return p. Line search direction:
```
def _try_update_with_direction(p, dir_p, n, L, best_p, best_fast, t_values):
   current = p
   best = p; best_score = _score_fast(p)
   for t in t_values:
      cand = (1-t)*p + t*dir_p
      cand = _normalize_to_one(cand)
      c = _fft_conv(cand,L); sc=...
      if sc < best_score: best=cand,best_score
   return best,best_score
```

Let's decide final structure:
1. Initialize best with one-sided and parent.
2. Run optimization loop until deadline-30:
   - If elapsed time since last greedy > 60s or stuck, run greedy for up to 20s, try directions.
   - Run Adam for up to 60s (or until deadline) using current best or a slightly perturbed p.
   - If Adam produced no improvement for 60s, perturb/restart.
This will spend most time Adam.

Simpler: Run Adam from best initial for full time. Also at start and end do greedy directions? But if Adam stuck, we miss. We can interleave.

Top-level `_run_adam` can take many parameters and returns updated. It can do exact evaluation occasionally? Up to return.

Let's define `_run_adam(v, n, L, deadline, rng, lr, mix, best_p, best_fast, best_exact, last_exact)` returning tuple.
Inside:
```
beta1=0.9; beta2=0.999; eps=1e-8
m=np.zeros(n); u=np.zeros(n); t=0
no_improve=0; last_score=best_fast
# To track current score for temperature, we store score_cur.
while time.monotonic()<deadline:
    p=_softmax(v)
    c=_fft_conv(p,L)
    score=2n*cmax
    if score < best_fast - 1e-12:
        best_fast=score; best_p=p.copy(); no_improve=0
        if score < best_exact - 1e-9 and time - last_exact > 5:
            escore = float(evaluate_sequence(best_p.tolist()))
            if escore < best_exact: best_exact=escore; last_exact=time
    else:
        no_improve += 1
    if no_improve > 3000:
        v = v + rng.normal(0, 0.05, n)
        lr = max(0.01, lr*0.7)
        mix = min(1.0, mix+0.1)
        no_improve=0
    # weights; maybe limit scale if cmax too small
    w=_make_weights(c, mix)
    g=_gradient_from_weights(w,p,L,n)
    g *= float(n)
    # normalize gradient to max 1 to stabilize
    gmax = float(np.max(np.abs(g)))
    if gmax > 1e-14:
       g = g / gmax
    t+=1
    m = beta1*m + (1-beta1)*g
    u = beta2*u + (1-beta2)*(g*g)
    mhat = m/(1-beta1**t)
    uhat = u/(1-beta2**t)
    v = v - lr * mhat/(np.sqrt(uhat)+eps)
    v = v - float(v.mean())
    vrange = float(v.max()-v.min())
    if not np.isfinite(v).all():
       break
    if vrange > 30:
       v = (v - float(v.mean())) * (30.0 / vrange)
    # adapt lr: if best_fast improved recently, increase; else decrease
    if no_improve == 0:
       lr = min(0.2, lr*1.02)
    else:
       lr = max(0.005, lr*0.995)
return v,m,u,t,best_p,best_fast,best_exact,last_exact
```
But we normalize g to max1 every iter; then lr is step in v units. Adaptive above. The `no_improve` counts iterations since any best score improvement. Since best score may improve every few iterations, lr may increase. Good.

However, normalizing g to max1 while using Adam: If gradient direction changes significantly, uhat adapts. The update magnitude per coordinate ~lr for high-gradient coords, less for low. This may be okay. But the mirror descent step for p may be too aggressive for coords with tail low gradient? Actually low gradient coords get smaller uhat, so update maybe larger relative? For g near zero, uhat small, mhat small; ratio m/sqrt(u) ~ direction sign with magnitude ~1 if persistent. So all coords with nonzero persistent gradient get similar updates? That might over-mix. Maybe don't normalize to max; use `g *= n` preserving scale, and let lr reflect. For one-sided p, grad max ~1, tail smaller; low tail gradient leads smaller update. Normalizing may make tail increase too much? Let's not normalize to max. Use `g *= float(n)` and optionally if gmax too large/small, scale to typical: maybe `g = g / (np.max(np.abs(g)) + 1e-12)`? Hmm.
We want step in v to be O(0.01-0.1) for important coords. Since grad_unit max ~1, lr=0.05 okay. If p has different scale, grad_unit max may vary but likely O(1) because weights sum1 and p has entries up to O(1/√n); sum over weights maybe O(1/√n)? Actually grad_unit=n*2∑w p; ∑w p ≤ max p? w sum1, p max could be 0.01 for singular? For p0 max=1/(2√n)=0.01; grad_unit max up to n*0.02? Wait ∑w p <= max p=0.01, times2n=0.5? O(1). If p has spike 0.1, grad_unit up to 2.5, still O(1). So no normalize okay. Use if gmax > 10, scale down to avoid huge? Could do `if gmax > 2: g*=2/gmax`. But not needed.
Let's use `g *= float(n)` only. lr start 0.02.

Adam moment with g scale O(1) gives updates O(lr). Good.

Top-level:
```
def _add_top(top, score, p, max_keep=12):
    if not np.isfinite(score): return
    top.append((score, p.copy()))
    top.sort(key=lambda x: x[0]) # but lambda banned? Don't use lambda functions. Use `top.sort(key=len?)` no. We can avoid sort with loop.
    # find max to remove if >max_keep
    if len(top)>max_keep:
       # find index of largest score
       max_idx=0; max_val=top[0][0]
       for i in range(1,len(top)):
           if top[i][0] > max_val: max_val=top[i][0]; max_idx=i
       top.pop(max_idx)
```

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1500, **kwargs):
    start = time.monotonic()

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    else:
        n=parent.size
    parent = _normalize_to_one(parent, n)
    Mlen = 2*n-1
    L=1
    while L < 3*n-1: L <<=1
    # initialize top list
    top=[]
    best_p=parent.copy(); best_fast=frozenscore; add_top
    # add one-sided candidates
    for alpha in ...:
       p0 = (np.arange(n)+1.0)**(-alpha); p0/_normalize
       score=...
       if score<best_fast: ...
       else add_top? Maybe add all with finite.
    # exact evaluate initial top to set best_exact
    best_exact=np.inf; exact_candidates = top + [(fast,parent)...]
    for sc,p in exact_candidates:
       es = _evaluate_p(p) # top-level calls evaluate_sequence
       if es < best_exact: best_exact=es; best_p=p
    # If best_p score > maybe from one-sided, good.
    # Optimization loop
    last_greedy=0.0
    run_idx=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 20.0:
       now=time.monotonic()
       # Decide slice
       slice_end = min(deadline - 20.0, now + 45.0)
       # Initial v from best_p
       p_safe = best_p + 1e-15
       p_safe = p_safe / max(1e-15,float(p_safe.sum()))
       v = np.log(p_safe)
       # Add perturb depending run_idx
       if run_idx == 0:
           # small random to break symmetry
           v += rng.normal(0.0, 0.01, n)
       else:
           sigma = 0.02 + 0.01*min(5, run_idx)
           v += rng.normal(0.0, sigma, n)
       v = v - float(v.mean())
       # hyperparameters
       lr = 0.02
       if run_idx % 3 == 1: lr=0.03
       elif run_idx % 3 == 2: lr=0.01
       mix = 0.4 + 0.1*(run_idx % 5)
       # Run Adam
       v, best_p, best_fast, best_exact = _run_adam(v, n, L, slice_end, rng, lr, mix, best_p, best_fast, best_exact, top)
       # After slice, if run_idx multiple, greedy
       if now + 45 > last_greedy: # roughly every 45s
           if time.monotonic() < deadline - 60.0:
               dir_p, dir_sum = _greedy_direction(best_p, L, n, rng, max_orders=8, passes=2, frac=0.8, timeout=deadline - 45.0)
               if dir_sum > 1.0 + 1e-8:

                   if newfast < best_fast - 1e-12:
                       best_p = newp; best_fast = newfast; _add_top(top, best_fast, best_p)
                       # maybe exact eval
                       es = _evaluate_p(best_p)
                       if es < best_exact: best_exact=es
       run_idx +=1
    # final exact evaluation of top + best
    candidates = {}
    # Since no dict? We can use list; exact duplicate small.
    exact_list = []
    # Add best_p, parent, one-sided alphas? top already has best initial maybe.
    for sc,p in top:
        exact_list.append(p)
    exact_list.append(best_p)
    exact_list.append(parent)
    # one-sided pure again
    x=np.arange(n)+1.0; p=x**-0.5; p/=p.sum(); exact_list.append(p)
    # Evaluate unique by maybe simple loop with seen? Could duplicate but fine (<=20).
    final_p=best_p; final_exact=best_exact
    for p in exact_list:
       if p is None: continue
       p=_normalize_to_one(p,n)

    # If final_exact inf, use fallback pure one-sided normalized
    if not np.isfinite(final_exact):

    return [float(x) for x in final_p]

def _evaluate_p(p):
    try:
        vals=[float(x) for x in np.asarray(p).tolist()]
        return float(evaluate_sequence(vals))
    except Exception:
        return float('inf')

Let's implement `_run_adam` top-level:
```
def _run_adam(v, n, L, slice_deadline, rng, lr, mix, best_p, best_fast, best_exact, top):
    beta1=0.9; beta2=0.999; eps=1e-8
    m=np.zeros(n); u=np.zeros(n); t=0
    no_improve=0
    last_exact=0.0
    current_mix=mix
    # To avoid exact too often, maybe check every 2s
    while time.monotonic() < slice_deadline:
       p=_softmax(v)
       c=_fft_conv(p,L)
       cmax=float(c.max())
       score=2.0*n*cmax
       if score < best_fast - 1e-12:
          best_fast=score; best_p=p.copy(); no_improve=0
          _add_top(top, best_fast, best_p)
          if best_fast < best_exact - 1e-9:
             nowt=time.monotonic()
             # exact expensive; if time - last_exact > 5
             if nowt - last_exact > 5.0:
                es=_evaluate_p(best_p)
                last_exact=nowt
                if np.isfinite(es) and es < best_exact:
                   best_exact=es
       else:
          no_improve +=1
       # periodic perturb
       if no_improve > 2000:
          v = v + rng.normal(0.0, 0.03, n)
          lr = max(0.005, lr*0.8)
          current_mix = min(1.0, current_mix + 0.1)
          no_improve=0
       # Build weights
       current_mix = min(1.0, current_mix + 0.00005)
       w=_make_weights(c, current_mix)
       g=_gradient_from_weights(w,p,L,n)
       g *= float(n)
       # If gradient has large max, scale to 2? avoid huge; if tiny, normalize to 1? Use safe
       gmax=float(np.max(np.abs(g)))
       if gmax > 2.0:
           g *= (2.0/gmax)
       elif gmax < 1e-8:
           g *= (1.0/max(gmax,1e-12))
       t +=1
       m = beta1*m + (1-beta1)*g
       u = beta2*u + (1-beta2)*(g*g)
       mhat = m/(1.0 - beta1**t)
       uhat = u/(1.0 - beta2**t)
       v = v - lr * mhat / (np.sqrt(uhat) + eps)
       v = v - float(v.mean())
       if not np.isfinite(v).any():? use all
       if not np.isfinite(v).all():
           break
       vrange = float(v.max() - v.min())
       if vrange > 30.0:
           v = (v - float(v.mean())) * (30.0 / vrange)
       # adapt lr
       if no_improve ==0:
           lr = min(0.25, lr * 1.01)
       else:
           lr = max(0.005, lr * 0.995)
    return v, best_p, best_fast, best_exact
```

def _try_direction_update(p, d, n, L):
   # d normalized sum1
   best_p=p; best_score=_score fast(p)
   t_values=(0.05,0.1,0.2,0.35,0.5,0.75,1.0)
   for t in t_values:
      cand = (1-t)*p + t*d
      cand = _normalize_to_one(cand)
      c=_fft_conv(cand,L); sc=...
      if sc < best_score:
         best=cand; best_score=sc
   return best, best_score

Greedy details top-level:
```
def _greedy_direction(p, L, n, rng, max_orders=8, passes=2, frac=0.8, timeout=None):
    c=_fft_conv(p,L); Mval=float(c.max())
    if Mval<=0: return p.copy(),1.0
    slacks_base = Mval - c
    slacks_base = np.where(slacks_base > 0.0, slacks_base, 0.0)
    invp = np.full(n, np.inf)
    pos = p > 1e-300
    invp[pos] = 1.0 / p[pos]
    best_g = p.copy(); best_sum=1.0
    idx=np.arange(n)
    orders=[]
    # Tail first, head first, ascending p, descending p, random
    orders.append(idx[::-1].copy())
    orders.append(idx.copy())
    orders.append(np.argsort(p).copy())
    orders.append(np.argsort(p)[::-1].copy())
    for _ in range(max(0, max_orders-4)):
        orders.append(rng.permutation(n).copy())
    for order in orders:
        if timeout is not None and time.monotonic() > timeout:
            break
        slacks = slacks_base.copy()
        g = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        for pass_no in range(passes):
            # For second pass maybe use same order reversed to try fill
            use_order = order if (pass_no %2==0) else order[::-1]
            for j in use_order:
                slice_slacks = slacks[j:j+n]
                # Need min of slice_slacks * invp
                # Use np.minimum.reduce? We can multiply in place? 
                ratio_slice = slice_slacks * invp
                # Replace nonfinite with inf
                if not ratio_slice.all(): # AttributeError; use isfinite
                    ratio_slice = np.where(np.isfinite(ratio_slice), ratio_slice, np.inf)
                d = float(np.min(ratio_slice))
                if d > 1e-15:
                    if frac < 1.0:
                        mult = 0.7 + 0.6 * float(rng.random())
                        d *= mult * frac
                    g[j] += d
                    upd = slice_slacks - p * d
                    slacks[j:j+n] = np.maximum(upd, 0.0)
        s=float(g.sum())
        if s > best_sum + 1e-12:
            best_sum=s
            best_g = g / s
    return best_g, best_sum
```

Let's draft top-level `_add_top` without lambda:
```
def _add_top(top, score, p, max_keep=16):
    if not np.isfinite(score): return
    top.append((float(score), p.copy()))
    if len(top) > max_keep:
       worst_idx=0; worst_val=top[0][0]
       for i in range(1,len(top)):
          if top[i][0] > worst_val:
              worst_val=top[i][0]; worst_idx=i
       top.pop(worst_idx)
```

def _fft_conv(p, L):
    P = np.fft.rfft(p, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(P * P, L)
    # Only first 2n-1 meaningful; but for max we can include all, zeros after.
    c = np.where(c > 0.0, c, 0.0)
    return c
```

Let's write top-level functions in final mentally:

```
import numpy as np
import time

def _normalize_to_one(p, n_fallback=2535):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    if p.ndim != 1:
        p = p.reshape(-1)
    if p.size == 0:
        p = np.ones(n_fallback, dtype=np.float64)
    p = np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)
    p = np.clip(p, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(p.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-15:
        p = np.ones(p.size if p.size else n_fallback, dtype=np.float64)
        s = float(p.sum())
    return p / s
```
If p has inf? where finite sets 0.

`_fft_conv` uses `np.fft.rfft` on p float64. If p contains inf? normalized no.

`_softmax`:
```
def _softmax(v):
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64)
    vmax = float(np.max(v))
    if not np.isfinite(vmax):
        return np.full(v.shape, 1.0/v.size)
    v = v - vmax
    e = np.exp(np.clip(v, -745.0, 0.0))
    s = float(e.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.full(v.shape, 1.0/v.size)
    return e / s
```

`_make_weights` as above.

`_gradient_from_weights` returns g length n. Maybe ensure finite:
```
g = _gradient...; if not all finite, fill 0.
```

```
def _evaluate_p(p):
    try:
       arr=np.asarray(p,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
       vals=[float(x) for x in arr]
       val=float(evaluate_sequence(vals))
       return val
    except Exception:
       return float('inf')
```
This creates list; maybe `arr.tolist()` faster:
```
vals = arr.tolist()
```

`_greedy_direction` use `pf` normalized.
```
def _greedy_direction(p, L, n, rng, max_orders=10, passes=2, frac=0.8):
    pf = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    pf = np.where(pf > 1e-12, pf, 1e-12)
    pf = pf / float(pf.sum())
    c = _fft_conv(pf, L)
    Mval = float(c.max())
    if Mval <= 0: return pf.copy(), 1.0
    slacks_base = Mval - c
    slacks_base = np.where(slacks_base > 0.0, slacks_base, 0.0)
    invpf = 1.0 / pf
    best_g = pf.copy(); best_sum=1.0
    idx=np.arange(n)
    orders=[]
    orders.append(idx[::-1].copy())
    orders.append(idx.copy())
    orders.append(np.argsort(pf).copy())
    orders.append(np.argsort(pf)[::-1].copy())
    extra = max(0, int(max_orders)-4)
    for _ in range(extra):
        orders.append(rng.permutation(n).copy())
    for order in orders:
        slacks = slacks_base.copy()
        g = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        for pass_no in range(passes):
            use = order if (pass_no == 0) else order[::-1]
            for j in use:
                ratio = slacks[j:j+n] * invpf
                # If slacks has tiny negative, ratio negative; min could negative.
                # We can clip slacks positive before multiply? ratio = np.maximum(slacks,0)*invpf
                # Since slacks updated clip, okay.
                d = float(np.min(ratio))
                if d > 1e-15:
                    if frac < 1.0:
                        d *= frac * (0.7 + 0.6 * float(rng.random()))
                    else:
                        d *= 1.0
                    g[j] += d
                    upd = slacks[j:j+n] - pf * d
                    slacks[j:j+n] = np.maximum(upd, 0.0)
        s = float(g.sum())
        if s > best_sum + 1e-12:
            best_sum = s
            best_g = g / s
    return best_g, best_sum
```
If `ratio` has NaN? slacks, invpf finite; no. If d negative due negative slacks not clipped, we skip. But we clip after update. Good.

def _make_weights(c, mix):
    cmax = float(c.max())
    m = c.size
    if cmax <= 0:
        return np.full(m, 1.0/m)
    scale = 8.0 / cmax
    u = (c - cmax) * scale
    u = np.clip(u, -745.0, 0.0)
    w_soft = np.exp(u)
    sw = float(w_soft.sum())
    if sw <=0 or not np.isfinite(sw):
        w_soft = np.zeros(m)
        w_soft[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
    else:
        w_soft /= sw
    csum = float(c.sum())
    if csum > 0 and np.isfinite(csum):
       w_lin = c / csum
    else:
       w_lin = np.full(m, 1.0/m)
    w = mix*w_soft + (1.0-mix)*w_lin
    ws = float(w.sum())
    if ws <= 0 or not np.isfinite(ws):
       return np.full(m,1.0/m)
    return w/ws
```
Mix can be >1? We pass <=1. If mix=1, w_soft only. Good.

def _gradient_from_weights(w, p, L, n):

    g *= 2.0
    g = np.where(np.isfinite(g), g, 0.0)
    return g

```python
"""
Search for a low-scoring nonnegative sequence.

Strategy:
- Start from the supplied construction and several one-sided square-root
  motivated initializations.
- Use FFT-based fast convolution for the internal objective.
- A softmax/mirror-descent Adam loop optimizes log-weights with adaptive
  weights focused on the largest convolution coefficients.
- A greedy LP-like direction (approximating the state-of-art LP step) is
  interleaved to make larger jumps.
- The best exact callers are verified with evaluate_sequence() before return.
"""

import numpy as np
import time


def _normalize_to_one(p, n_fallback=2535):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    if p.ndim != 1:
        p = p.reshape(-1)
    if p.size == 0:
        p = np.ones(n_fallback, dtype=np.float64)
    p = np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)
    p = np.clip(p, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(p.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-15:
        p = np.ones(p.size if p.size else n_fallback, dtype=np.float64)
        s = float(p.sum())
    return p / s


def _fft_conv(p, L):
    P = np.fft.rfft(p, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(P * P, L)
    c = np.where(c > 0.0, c, 0.0)
    return c


def _score_fast(p, n, L):
    c = _fft_conv(p, L)
    return 2.0 * float(n) * float(c.max())


def _softmax(v):
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64)
    if v.size == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    vmax = float(np.max(v))
    if not np.isfinite(vmax):
        return np.full(v.shape, 1.0 / float(v.size), dtype=np.float64)
    v = v - vmax
    e = np.exp(np.clip(v, -745.0, 0.0))
    s = float(e.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.full(v.shape, 1.0 / float(v.size), dtype=np.float64)
    return e / s


def _make_weights(c, mix):
    m = c.size
    cmax = float(c.max())
    if cmax <= 0.0 or m == 0:
        return np.full(m, 1.0 / max(1, m), dtype=np.float64)

    scale = 8.0 / cmax
    u = (c - cmax) * scale
    u = np.clip(u, -745.0, 0.0)
    w_soft = np.exp(u)
    sw = float(w_soft.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(sw) or sw <= 0.0:
        w_soft = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)
        w_soft[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
    else:
        w_soft = w_soft / sw

    csum = float(c.sum())
    if np.isfinite(csum) and csum > 0.0:
        w_lin = c / csum
    else:
        w_lin = np.full(m, 1.0 / max(1, m), dtype=np.float64)

    w = mix * w_soft + (1.0 - mix) * w_lin
    ws = float(w.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(ws) or ws <= 0.0:
        return np.full(m, 1.0 / max(1, m), dtype=np.float64)
    return w / ws


def _gradient_from_weights(w, p, L, n):
    pr = p[::-1]
    W = np.fft.rfft(w, L)
    P = np.fft.rfft(pr, L)
    g = np.fft.irfft(W * P, L)
    g = g[n - 1:2 * n - 1]
    g = 2.0 * g
    g = np.where(np.isfinite(g), g, 0.0)
    return g


def _evaluate_p(p):
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        return float(evaluate_sequence(arr.tolist()))
    except Exception:
        return float("inf")


def _add_top(top, score, p, max_keep=20):
    if not np.isfinite(score):
        return
    top.append((float(score), np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).copy()))
    if len(top) > max_keep:
        worst_idx = 0
        worst_val = top[0][0]
        for i in range(1, len(top)):
            if top[i][0] > worst_val:
                worst_val = top[i][0]
                worst_idx = i
        top.pop(worst_idx)


def _greedy_direction(p, L, n, rng, max_orders=12, passes=2, frac=0.8):
    pf = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    pf = np.where(pf > 1e-12, pf, 1e-12)
    pf = pf / float(pf.sum())

    c = _fft_conv(pf, L)
    mval = float(c.max())
    if mval <= 0.0:
        return pf.copy(), 1.0

    slacks_base = mval - c
    slacks_base = np.where(slacks_base > 0.0, slacks_base, 0.0)
    invpf = 1.0 / pf

    best_g = pf.copy()
    best_sum = 1.0

    idx = np.arange(n)
    orders = []
    orders.append(idx[::-1].copy())
    orders.append(idx.copy())
    orders.append(np.argsort(pf).copy())
    orders.append(np.argsort(pf)[::-1].copy())
    extra = max(0, int(max_orders) - 4)
    for _ in range(extra):
        orders.append(rng.permutation(n).copy())

    for order in orders:
        slacks = slacks_base.copy()
        g = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

        for pass_no in range(passes):
            use = order if pass_no == 0 else order[::-1]
            for j in use:
                ratio = slacks[j:j + n] * invpf
                d = float(np.min(ratio))
                if d > 1e-15:
                    if frac < 1.0:
                        d *= frac * (0.7 + 0.6 * float(rng.random()))
                    g[j] += d
                    upd = slacks[j:j + n] - pf * d
                    slacks[j:j + n] = np.maximum(upd, 0.0)

        s = float(g.sum())
        if s > best_sum + 1e-12:
            best_sum = s
            best_g = g / s

    return best_g, best_sum


def _try_direction_update(p, d, n, L):
    p = _normalize_to_one(p)
    d = _normalize_to_one(d)

    best_p = p.copy()
    best_score = _score_fast(p, n, L)

    t_values = (0.02, 0.05, 0.10, 0.20, 0.35, 0.50, 0.75, 1.00)
    for t in t_values:
        cand = (1.0 - t) * p + t * d
        cand = _normalize_to_one(cand)
        sc = _score_fast(cand, n, L)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best_p = cand.copy()

    return best_p, best_score


def _run_adam(v, n, L, slice_deadline, rng, lr, mix, best_p, best_fast, best_exact, top):
    beta1 = 0.9
    beta2 = 0.999
    eps = 1e-8

    m = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    u = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    t = 0
    no_improve = 0
    current_mix = float(mix)
    last_exact = time.monotonic()

    while time.monotonic() < slice_deadline:
        p = _softmax(v)
        c = _fft_conv(p, L)
        score = 2.0 * float(n) * float(c.max())

        if np.isfinite(score) and score < best_fast - 1e-12:
            best_fast = score
            best_p = p.copy()
            no_improve = 0
            _add_top(top, best_fast, best_p)

            if best_fast < best_exact - 1e-9 and (time.monotonic() - last_exact) > 10.0:
                es = _evaluate_p(best_p)
                last_exact = time.monotonic()
                if np.isfinite(es) and es < best_exact:
                    best_exact = es
        else:
            no_improve += 1

        if no_improve > 2000:
            v = v + rng.normal(0.0, 0.03, n)
            lr = max(0.005, lr * 0.8)
            current_mix = min(1.0, current_mix + 0.1)
            no_improve = 0

        current_mix = min(1.0, current_mix + 0.00005)
        w = _make_weights(c, current_mix)
        g = _gradient_from_weights(w, p, L, n)
        g *= float(n)

        gmax = float(np.max(np.abs(g)))
        if not np.isfinite(gmax) or gmax <= 0.0:
            break
        if gmax > 2.0:
            g *= (2.0 / gmax)
        elif gmax < 1e-8:
            g *= (1.0 / gmax)

        t += 1
        m = beta1 * m + (1.0 - beta1) * g
        u = beta2 * u + (1.0 - beta2) * (g * g)

        mhat = m / (1.0 - beta1 ** t)
        uhat = u / (1.0 - beta2 ** t)

        v = v - lr * mhat / (np.sqrt(uhat) + eps)
        v = v - float(v.mean())

        if not np.all(np.isfinite(v)):
            break

        vrange = float(v.max() - v.min())
        if vrange > 30.0:
            v = (v - float(v.mean())) * (30.0 / vrange)

        if no_improve == 0:
            lr = min(0.25, lr * 1.01)
        else:
            lr = max(0.005, lr * 0.995)

    return v, best_p, best_fast, best_exact


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1500, **kwargs):
    start = time.monotonic()
    budget = min(float(budget_s), 1500.0)
    if budget < 120.0:
        work_deadline = start + max(0.0, budget - 2.0)
    else:
        work_deadline = start + budget - 90.0

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    parent = None
    try:
        parent = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64)
    except Exception:
        parent = None

    if parent is None or parent.ndim != 1 or parent.size == 0 or not np.all(np.isfinite(parent)):
        n = 2535
        parent = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        n = int(parent.size)

    parent = _normalize_to_one(parent, n)
    M = 2 * n - 1
    L = 1
    while L < 3 * n - 1:
        L <<= 1

    top = []
    best_p = parent.copy()
    best_fast = _score_fast(parent, n, L)
    best_exact = float("inf")
    _add_top(top, best_fast, parent)

    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 1.0

    initial_candidates = []
    for alpha in (0.45, 0.50, 0.55, 0.60):
        initial_candidates.append(x ** (-alpha))

    # Mild same-side corrections.
    base = x ** (-0.5)
    for beta_exp, frac in ((0.30, 0.04), (0.35, 0.08), (0.40, 0.12)):
        initial_candidates.append(base + frac * (x ** (-beta_exp)))

    for cand in initial_candidates:
        p0 = _normalize_to_one(cand, n)
        sc = _score_fast(p0, n, L)
        if np.isfinite(sc):
            _add_top(top, sc, p0)
            if sc < best_fast:
                best_fast = sc
                best_p = p0.copy()

    for sc, p0 in list(top):
        es = _evaluate_p(p0)
        if np.isfinite(es) and es < best_exact:
            best_exact = es
            best_p = p0.copy()

    print("initial fast=%.6f exact=%.6f" % (best_fast, best_exact))

    run_idx = 0
    while time.monotonic() < work_deadline:
        now = time.monotonic()
        slice_deadline = min(work_deadline, now + 35.0)

        p_base = best_p
        p_safe = p_base + 1e-15
        p_safe = p_safe / float(p_safe.sum())
        v = np.log(p_safe)

        if run_idx == 0:
            sigma = 0.01
            lr = 0.05
            mix = 0.30
        else:
            sigma = 0.02 + 0.004 * min(12, run_idx)
            lr = 0.04 + 0.02 * (1 if (run_idx % 2 == 0) else 0)
            mix = 0.25 + 0.10 * (run_idx % 4)

        v = v + rng.normal(0.0, sigma, n)
        v = v - float(v.mean())

        v, best_p, best_fast, best_exact = _run_adam(
            v, n, L, slice_deadline, rng, lr, mix, best_p, best_fast, best_exact, top
        )

        if run_idx % 5 == 0 and time.monotonic() < work_deadline - 25.0:
            dir_p, dir_sum = _greedy_direction(best_p, L, n, rng, max_orders=12, passes=2, frac=0.8)
            if dir_sum > 1.0 + 1e-8:
                newp, newfast = _try_direction_update(best_p, dir_p, n, L)
                if newfast < best_fast - 1e-12:
                    best_p = newp
                    best_fast = newfast
                    _add_top(top, best_fast, best_p)
                    es = _evaluate_p(best_p)
                    if np.isfinite(es) and es < best_exact:
                        best_exact = es

        if run_idx % 8 == 0:
            print("t=%.1f fast=%.6f exact=%.6f" % (time.monotonic() - start, best_fast, best_exact))

        run_idx += 1

    # Final exact verification over the most promising fast-score candidates.
    candidates = []
    for _, p0 in top:
        candidates.append(p0)
    candidates.append(best_p)
    candidates.append(parent)
    pure = x ** (-0.5)
    pure = pure / float(pure.sum())
    candidates.append(pure)

    final_p = best_p
    final_exact = best_exact
    for p0 in candidates:
        p0 = _normalize_to_one(p0, n)
        es = _evaluate_p(p0)
        if np.isfinite(es) and es < final_exact:
            final_exact = es
            final_p = p0

    if not np.all(np.isfinite(final_p)) or float(final_p.sum()) <= 0.0:
        final_p = pure
        final_exact = _evaluate_p(final_p)

    final_p = _normalize_to_one(final_p, n)
    return [float(v) for v in final_p]
```
